Ran cd /mnt/user-data/outputs && wc -l writing-library.ts && echo "---" && head -20 writing-library.ts
Ran cd /mnt/user-data/outputs && cat > writing-library.ts << 'ENDOFFILE' /* ============================================================================= * $Chemistry WRITING LIBRARY — running declarations * ============================================================================= * * WHAT THIS IS * This file is wound forward alongside the design conversation, as an event * stream. It is NOT a settled specification. Members get added, renamed and * deleted as the stream advances, and the comments record why. An implementer * should read it top to bottom in stream order before writing real code. * * STREAM POSITION: through "Section decides to be closed." * ("A permissive but closed composition allows all sorts of writing, but * nothing outside of it. This is all specified in its specification.") * Everything later in the conversation is NOT yet integrated. * * PROVENANCE RULE * Quoted lines marked [D] are Doug's words, verbatim. They are the * specification. Unquoted commentary is Claude's reading of them and has been * wrong repeatedly in this conversation. Where Claude's reading is load-bearing * it is marked [inference] so it can be checked or discarded. * * VOCABULARY RULE * Only vocabulary Doug used is permitted here. Claude introduced a large amount * of outside vocabulary earlier in the conversation and withdrew it on request. * Do not reintroduce it when extending this file. * * NAMING CONVENTION IN THIS FILE * When a class gains or changes members partway through the stream, it is * restated with an _E<n> suffix naming the event that revised it. The suffix is * an artifact of the stream, not part of the library. Winding fully forward and * collapsing the _E<n> restatements gives the real class. * * =========================================================================== */ /* ----------------------------------------------------------------------------- * EVENT 1 — Text * ----------------------------------------------------------------------------- * [D] "Start with me at the text level. All it does it take text. Nothing else * is allowed." * [D] "<Text>Whatever as long as its not a component in this library.</Text>" * [D] "You can basically write all react in there as long as it is not * recursive text." * * STATUS: renamed to Letter at EVENT 3. Kept here only as history. * --------------------------------------------------------------------------- */ // class Text { ... } // RENAMED -> Letter (EVENT 3) /* ----------------------------------------------------------------------------- * EVENT 2 — Writing; Text becomes a type of Writing * ----------------------------------------------------------------------------- * [D] "Text then specifies. He evolves into Writing. Writing is specified to * only take other types of writing. Text is a type of writing. But he * overrides the specification to allow his original specification, making * him a sort of null writing" * [D] "Now we have writing, and anything out of this model constrained to a * special location" * --------------------------------------------------------------------------- */ /** * The root of the library. Everything the library comprehends is Writing. * * SPECIFICATION * 1. children are Writing. * * Subclasses override this specification. Letter overrides it to the inverse * (EVENT 3); the specification is per-class, not global. */ declare abstract class Writing { /** * Constructor arguments, per $Chemistry (EVENT 11). NOT placements. * (At this stream position they are simply "the children of the tag".) */ readonly children: unknown[]; /** Checked in the specification, not enforced at construction. */ protected abstract specification(): void; } /* ----------------------------------------------------------------------------- * EVENT 3 — Text renamed Letter * ----------------------------------------------------------------------------- * [D] "Text is like a letter, except... as a letter, it is allowed to have * anything" * [D] "Let's rename text <Letter> / As if you were writing one" * --------------------------------------------------------------------------- */ /** * Letter is Writing that inverts Writing's specification. * * SPECIFICATION * 1. takes anything that is NOT a component of this library. * 2. takes no Letter (no recursive text). * 3. level 0 (assigned EVENT 4; see the OPEN note below). * 4. then Annotation only (added EVENT 5). * * The contents are not the library's business. They render; the library has no * standing inside them. * * [OPEN — needs Doug] Letter's level. * EVENT 4 "he claims Letter as Level 0" * EVENT 6 "level gets subscript 0" * EVENT 7 "don't forget Letter has subscript 0" * EVENT 12 "So if Letter is level 1, then all of Letter - Paragraph ..." * EVENT 13 "The first 0-3 levels are all like one intermixed level" * [later] "like a Letter is the canonical level 1" * Written here as 0, since Word already holds 1 and 0-3 counts four levels. * If Letter and Word share 1, strict admission arithmetic changes below Section. * * [resolved later in stream — EVENT 12's openness removes the question of * whether the no-library-components rule is lexical or about what renders. * Do not try to settle that here.] */ declare class Letter extends Writing { /** Renamed from `subscript` at EVENT 10. See Composition for the history. */ readonly level: 0; } /* ----------------------------------------------------------------------------- * EVENT 4 — Composition * ----------------------------------------------------------------------------- * [D] "Let us then say writing specializes again. Let's make a new type of * Writing. Composition. Composition has a strange rule. It has a level, * and it only allows things in it that are at or below it in level except * at the end. After that it can be non-composition writing. Composition * splits the world for some reason." * [D] "And he claims Letter as Level 0" * [D] "Composition is weird but well formed" * * The trailing region ("non-composition writing") is narrowed at EVENT 5. * --------------------------------------------------------------------------- */ /** * SPECIFICATION (as of EVENT 4; item 2 superseded at EVENT 5) * 1. a leading region of children at or below this Composition's level, * plus Letter (level 0); * 2. then a trailing region of Writing that is not Composition; * 3. the transition is one-way. */ declare abstract class Composition extends Writing { /** * NAMING HISTORY — renamed three times across the stream: * EVENT 4 "It has a level" -> level * EVENT 5 "level evolves into rank as per convention" -> rank * EVENT 6 "rank converts to subscript!" -> subscript * EVENT 10 "Subscript goes back to level. Whoops" -> level (current) * * [D, EVENT 7] "These can all be changed dynamically, though they shouldn't * be. We can pretend that they can be assigned but not overridden." */ readonly level: number; } /* ----------------------------------------------------------------------------- * EVENT 5 — Annotation; the trailing region narrows * ----------------------------------------------------------------------------- * [D] "Writing and Composition agree that Composition is being too permissive * with Writing. So writing has a child called Annotation, and those are * the only ones allowed at the end. Any particular composition can be * annotated" * [D] "Even level 0 shouldn't be left out. The end of the Letter can be * annotations." * [D] "everyone adjusts" * [D] "And level evolves into rank as per convention" * [D] "This is a polymorphic framework btw, so actually, we have a lot more * options" * --------------------------------------------------------------------------- */ /** * SPECIFICATION (as of EVENT 5) * 1. Annotation is Writing, so it takes Writing. * * Attaches at every node at every level under the same rule. Not graded — see * the correction at EVENT 9, and the property split at EVENT 16 (not yet * integrated: parenthetical is on Writing, level is on grading). * * WHAT ANNOTATION IS NOT — see EVENT 9. Claude read this as commentary, * footnote, gloss and citation and was corrected. Those belong in the margin. */ declare class Annotation extends Writing {} /** * Revised specifications after "everyone adjusts": * * Composition * 1. leading region: children at or below this Composition's level, plus Letter * 2. trailing region: Annotation only * 3. one-way transition * * Letter * 1. contents: anything not of this library, no Letter * 2. then: Annotation only */ /* ----------------------------------------------------------------------------- * EVENT 6 — Word, Sentence, Paragraph; strict / permissive * ----------------------------------------------------------------------------- * [D] "Composition doesn't like his letter exposed. ... Composition how has a * few children. He simply decides <Word> <Sentence> <Paragraph>" * [D] "These are subscript 1, 2 & 3 respectively and level gets subscript 0, * and rank converts to subscript! The writing word is better for this * story and it gives math indexing" * [D] "But Composition also is feeling rigid. He decides to be * <Composition strict> or <Composition permissive>" * [D] "Permissive means at or below. Strict means at or one below" * [D] "All three of those are permissive" * [D] "These are his basic children. No one needs to use 0-3, unless they need * strict. They have a semantic family that they can use informally to * specialize in different directions" * --------------------------------------------------------------------------- */ type Admission = "strict" | "permissive"; declare abstract class Composition_E6 extends Composition { /** * [D, EVENT 7] "It's a property." * * permissive: child level m <= this level n * strict: child level m in { n - 1, n } * * Under permissive the level values are never consulted, only the relation. * Under strict the distance matters. [inference] */ readonly admission: Admission; } declare class Word extends Composition { readonly level: 1; readonly admission: "permissive"; } declare class Sentence extends Composition { readonly level: 2; readonly admission: "permissive"; } declare class Paragraph extends Composition { readonly level: 3; readonly admission: "permissive"; } /** * THE SEMANTIC FAMILY * [D] "They have a semantic family that they can use informally to specialize * in different directions" * * Informal naming at a given level. Claude invented family member names here * and was told they are not part of this model. Do not populate this with * invented names. * * [resolved later in stream] whether family members are distinct classes or * names over a level — it matters for `parts`, because the splice rule turns * on sameness. */ /* ----------------------------------------------------------------------------- * EVENT 7a — parts, and depth * ----------------------------------------------------------------------------- * [D] "Now, Composition realizes that he has parts. The parts of the * composition. And he realizes that recursion in a composition is special. * There is an emergent form of depth, a new computed property. If a * Composition returns his parts, he does not return his recursive one. He * reaches in and gets their parts." * --------------------------------------------------------------------------- */ declare abstract class Composition_E7 extends Composition_E6 { /** * The body of the composition. * * SPECIFICATION (as of EVENT 7; revised at EVENT 12) * 1. a recursive child is not returned; its parts are returned in its place. * * [OPEN at this position] what "recursive one" means — same level, or same * class. The two agree until the semantic family is used. * [resolved later in stream — do not decide it here.] */ parts(): Writing[]; /** * [D] "There is an emergent form of depth, a new computed property." * * Computed, not declared. A Composition does not know its own depth by * assignment; it is a fact about where it ended up. [inference] * * Revised at EVENT 10. */ readonly depth: number; } /* ----------------------------------------------------------------------------- * EVENT 7b — Section and Heading; the first reference * ----------------------------------------------------------------------------- * [D] "Now a pair comes into being: * Section : Composition, subscript 4, permissive * Heading: Sentence * They will be a form of twin" * [D] "The section has a canonical first member called a Heading. The heading * takes text or what text takes and wraps in text, as its specification, * and reaches to his parent section for his depth. Sections expect to be * nested" * [D] "Now, the first form of reference begins to emerge. The canonical Heading * means the section" * --------------------------------------------------------------------------- */ /** * SPECIFICATION (as of EVENT 7b; extended at EVENT 13) * 1. level 4 * 2. permissive * 3. first part is a Heading * 4. expects to be nested * * [D, EVENT 19] "Of course a canonical is required. That is a silly question. * The result would be a void." — so the canonical is not a check a Section can * fail. A Section without one is a void. Integrated here because it is a * direct answer about this member. * * [OPEN — needs Doug] whether a second Sentence in that position is anything. * Claude asserted it is "just writing in the Section"; that was Claude's * inference and was never confirmed. */ declare class Section extends Composition { readonly level: 4; readonly admission: "permissive"; readonly heading: Heading; } /** * [D] "The heading takes text or what text takes and wraps in text, as its * specification, and reaches to his parent section for his depth." * * SPECIFICATION * 1. takes text, or what text takes * 2. wraps in text * 3. has a parent Section * * The wrap is why Writing and Composition object at EVENT 10 ("he creates the * letter"). It gains a declared home at EVENT 11 (catalytic constructor) and * arguably stops being necessary at EVENT 12 (openness) — see the note there, * which is Claude's inference and was never confirmed. * * REFERENCE: the Heading means its Section. The binding is the position — * nothing is written into the Heading that names the Section. [inference, but * directly from "The canonical Heading means the section"] */ declare class Heading extends Sentence { /** * [D] "reaches to his parent section for his depth" * * [OPEN — needs Doug] whether this is the Section's depth, or one more. * They differ by one everywhere. */ readonly depth: number; } /* ----------------------------------------------------------------------------- * EVENT 8 — checkpoint, no declarations * ----------------------------------------------------------------------------- * [D] "So where are we? Is this a sort of writing? Are we making a object model * that has elements of a writing model? We have a ways to go" * * No specification content. Recorded to keep event numbering aligned with the * conversation. * --------------------------------------------------------------------------- */ /* ============================================================================= * ============================ PART 2 BEGINS HERE ============================= * ========================================================================== */ /* ----------------------------------------------------------------------------- * EVENT 9 — what Annotation actually is * ----------------------------------------------------------------------------- * [D] "Okay but those annotations are not annotations in that way. Those belong * in the margin. These annotations will be more like where types, * references, meaning lie" * * REVISES: the commentary on Annotation at EVENT 5. Annotation is not * commentary, footnote, gloss, citation or editorial note. Those are margin. * Annotation is where types, references and meaning live. * * NAMES BUT DOES NOT SPECIFY: `margin`. * [OPEN — needs Doug] margin is named here and, as far as the stream goes, * never given a type, a level, a property or a home. Format (EVENT 23) and * Theme (EVENT 24) arrive later and are not margin. If margin is meant to * exist as a component, nothing has been said about it. * --------------------------------------------------------------------------- */ declare class Annotation_E9 extends Annotation { /** * SPECIFICATION (as of EVENT 9) * 1. is Writing, so it takes Writing. * 2. carries types, references and meaning. What those are is not yet * specified; the first concrete one is Mention/Referent at EVENT 21. * * Because Annotation is Writing, the recursion reopens through an annotation: * an annotation can contain Compositions, therefore Letters, therefore * further Annotations. [inference — stated by Claude, not contradicted] */ } /* ----------------------------------------------------------------------------- * EVENT 10 — back matter; the level rename; depth becomes fundamental; * the composition function * ----------------------------------------------------------------------------- * [D] "The tail is back matter. It is the writing if you consider it the back * about the book and not a part of it. They are not visible in the * writing." * [D] "Your annotations are annotations at other levels." * [D] "Subscript goes back to level. Whoops" * [D] "Depth evolves to be a fundamental property of composition. Recursion now * has meaning in this system and it can be checked against ones parent. * Level and depth lend a sort of geometry" * [D] "And then something strange happens. Writing and Composition come to talk * to Heading, concerned about the way that he ignores Letter and he has no * answer. He creates the letter." * [D] "They don't like this, and decide to allow a new form of composition, * where the new component takes the inputs to first, runs the bond * constructor of one then the other, and returns them assembled in a * parent child relationship. A new composition function is added." * [D] "They are concerned about this seeming end to polymorphism, sense the * tension and sit down to ponder" * --------------------------------------------------------------------------- */ /* RENAME — applied throughout this file from here forward: * subscript -> level * Letter.level, Composition.level, Word/Sentence/Paragraph/Section.level. */ /* THE TAIL IS BACK MATTER. * This is a correction of Claude's reading, not a new rule. The trailing * Annotation region is: * - about the book, * - not a part of it, * - not visible in the writing. * Claude had read the tail as material that stopped being ranked. It is not. * * "Your annotations are annotations at other levels" — one Annotation type * whose reading varies by the level it attaches to. Not two kinds. */ declare abstract class Composition_E10 extends Composition_E7 { /** * [D] "Depth evolves to be a fundamental property of composition. Recursion * now has meaning in this system and it can be checked against ones * parent." * * SPECIFICATION * 1. depth is checkable against the parent. * * [inference] the natural reading of "checked against ones parent" is that a * Composition whose parent is a recursive one has the parent's depth plus * one, and otherwise starts over. This is Claude's reading of the check, not * Doug's statement of it, and it interacts with the OPEN question on * Heading.depth. Do not implement it without confirming. * * [D] "Level and depth lend a sort of geometry" — level is declared and * non-increasing downward; depth is counted and increases only under * recursion. [the second half is inference] */ readonly depth: number; } /** * THE COMPOSITION FUNCTION * [D] "a new form of composition, where the new component takes the inputs to * first, runs the bond constructor of one then the other, and returns them * assembled in a parent child relationship. A new composition function is * added." * * First appearance of `bond constructor` as a named thing. Its semantics arrive * at EVENT 11. * * SPECIFICATION * 1. takes the inputs to the first component * 2. runs the bond constructor of one, then the other * 3. returns them assembled parent-child * * Its motivating use is Heading's manufacture of a Letter. That motivation is * removed at EVENT 11 (the wrap gains a declared home) and arguably again at * EVENT 12. * * [OPEN — needs Doug] whether this composition function survives as a member of * the library in its own right, or is subsumed by the catalytic constructor * (EVENT 11), which can express it and more. Claude claimed the latter; Doug * did not confirm. */ declare function compose<A extends Writing, B extends Writing>( inner: new (...args: never[]) => A, outer: new (...args: never[]) => B, ): new (...args: never[]) => B; /* THE TENSION, RECORDED UNRESOLVED * [D] "They are concerned about this seeming end to polymorphism, sense the * tension and sit down to ponder" * Recorded as stated. Claude's analysis of what the tension was is not * specification and is omitted. */ /* ----------------------------------------------------------------------------- * EVENT 11 — $Chemistry; the catalytic constructor * ----------------------------------------------------------------------------- * [D] "Can we imagine a formula constructor, like the bond constructor, but for * composition? Perhaps it takes inputs and returns the React nodes that * will be fed to the bond constructor." * [D] "In case you don't know, in the $Chemistry wrapper for React that we * made, props act like properties of an object and the children act like * constructor arguments to the object at mount and the object graph is * reactive!" * [D] "This... maybe its a catalytic constructor if it's facillitating the * reaction in which bonding happens, but take the TSX, modify it, and give * it to the bond constructor, making that object graph and this runs as * part of mount" * [D] "A serious evolution, but sometimes even the laws of chemistry bend to * evolution" * [D] "And now a heading has a place to declare it wraps text. A piece of * writing could concatenate two others with the same inputs. Powerful" * --------------------------------------------------------------------------- */ /** * THE $Chemistry CONTRACT — stated by Doug, not invented here. * 1. props act like properties of an object * 2. children act like constructor arguments to the object at mount * 3. the object graph is reactive * * Consequence stated later at EVENT 25: "The child input to a chemical is a * constructor argument not a placement." A chemical renders what it holds * wherever it decides to. */ declare abstract class Chemical { /** * Turns constructor arguments into the object and bonds it into the reactive * object graph. * * [OPEN at this position] whether the bond constructor checks admission. * Claude repeatedly assumed it does. Nothing at this position says so, and * EVENT 20 ("The specification only runs in dev mode") suggests it does not. * Do not write validation into this until that lands in Part 3. */ protected bondConstructor(...args: unknown[]): void; /** * SPECIFICATION * 1. takes the inputs * 2. returns the React nodes that will be fed to the bond constructor * 3. runs as part of mount * * Takes TSX, modifies it, gives it to the bond constructor. The catalyst * proposes structure; the bond constructor builds it. * * SYNTAX: `$$`, per EVENT 12. * [D, EVENT 17] "$$ catalysis.. yes it catalyzes the reaction. Bonding still * happens." — integrated here because it names this member. * * NAMED AGAIN LATER: at EVENT 45 Doug reconsiders — "Or perhaps it is the * formulator!" Not integrated yet. */ protected catalyticConstructor?(...args: unknown[]): unknown; } declare class Heading_E11 extends Heading { /** * [D] "And now a heading has a place to declare it wraps text." * * The wrap moves out of Heading's insides and into a declared catalytic * constructor. The Letter is still manufactured; the manufacture is now * declared. */ protected catalyticConstructor(...args: unknown[]): unknown; } /** * [D] "A piece of writing could concatenate two others with the same inputs." * Recorded as a capability of the catalytic constructor. No class declared for * it; Doug named the capability, not a component. */ /* ----------------------------------------------------------------------------- * EVENT 12 — open / closed; foreign content as whitespace; $$ * ----------------------------------------------------------------------------- * [D] "You know what? This is simply a problem of not applying permissive to * the inputs of text. So if Letter is level 1, then all of Letter - * Paragraph now can take anything react, writing, strings!" * [D] "Composition evolves a new boolean pair called open and closed, one being * a computed settable property that behaves as the opposite of the other. * Open means react is allowed inside and closed does it allow it." * [reads as: closed does NOT allow it] * [D] "Parts will now only enumerate the children that Writing, as if the react * and strings are whitespace around the real writing." * [D] "This has become a form of metaphorical writing where if a Letter * contained a whole book as a string, that would represent but one letter. * Profound!" * [D] "But Writing still decides to let its substrate evolve. Annotation * suggests that many occupying it will want to insert themselves. So we * gain new syntax for the new constructor which has an extra $ relative to * the bond constructor. It's a tough feature but $Chemistry has much DOM * handling and it works" * * Doug's example at this event (note `level=4`, confirming the EVENT 10 * rename): * * <Writing> * <Composition open level=4> * Text <React>of any kind</React> * <Sentence> * Annotation * <Letter> * Text <React>of any kind</React> * <Annotation> * <Paragraph /> * Text <React>of any kind</React> * <Annotation> * <Annotation> * <Annotation> * --------------------------------------------------------------------------- */ declare abstract class Composition_E12 extends Composition_E10 { /** * SPECIFICATION * 1. open and closed are a boolean pair * 2. one is a computed settable property that behaves as the opposite of * the other * 3. open means react is allowed inside * 4. closed means it is not * * Which of the two is the stored one and which is computed is an * implementation choice; setting either must set the other's inverse. * * [OPEN — needs Doug] where this pair lives. Doug says "Composition evolves" * it, but the same sentence puts Letter in the range that can now take react * ("all of Letter - Paragraph"). Letter is not a Composition. Is Letter open * by carrying this property, or by its own EVENT 3 specification? The same * kind of question Doug answered precisely at EVENT 16 for parenthetical * ("parenthetical is on writing. level is on grading"). */ readonly open: boolean; readonly closed: boolean; } declare abstract class Composition_E12_parts extends Composition_E12 { /** * SPECIFICATION (revised at EVENT 12) * 1. enumerates only the children that are Writing * 2. react and strings are as whitespace around the real writing * 3. a recursive child is not returned; its parts are returned in its place * * CONSEQUENCE, stated by Doug: "if a Letter contained a whole book as a * string, that would represent but one letter." The measure of how much * writing is present depends on declared structure, not on content volume. * * [revised again shortly after this position] Doug corrects the framing: * parts does not filter strings, it operates in a space where they are not * members; and it skips annotations too. The term `calligraphy` arrives with * that correction. Both land in Part 3. Do not pre-apply them. */ parts(): Writing[]; } /** * $$ — the new constructor syntax * [D] "we gain new syntax for the new constructor which has an extra $ relative * to the bond constructor" * [D] "It's a tough feature but $Chemistry has much DOM handling and it works" * * MOTIVATING CASE at this event: "Annotation suggests that many occupying it * will want to insert themselves." * * [D, EVENT 17] "$$ catalysis.. yes it catalyzes the reaction. Bonding still * happens." — so $$ is the syntax for the catalytic constructor of EVENT 11, * and everything it produces still goes through bonding. * * [revised later] at EVENT 21 the annotation collection is built in the bond * constructor from declared types, and Doug notes that Quote "no longer has the * problem of needing catalysis." The insertion motivation for $$ weakens there. * Not integrated yet. */ declare class Heading_E12 extends Heading_E11 { /** * [inference — NOT Doug's statement] With openness, a Sentence can take text * directly, so Heading may no longer need to wrap in a Letter at all. Claude * asserted this; Doug neither confirmed nor denied it, and moved on to * EVENT 13. Flagged so an implementer does not remove the wrap on Claude's * say-so. */ } /* ----------------------------------------------------------------------------- * EVENT 13 — Section becomes closed; the 0-3 band; specification is universal * ----------------------------------------------------------------------------- * [D] "Section decides to be closed. Having a canonical reference compels it to * purity, making paragraph an emergent sort of letter of its own kind. The * first 0-3 levels are all like one intermixed level. A permissive but * closed composition allows all sorts of writing, but nothing outside of * it. This is all specified in its specification. All pieces of writing * are subject to their specification and we have been quietly building * this" * --------------------------------------------------------------------------- */ declare class Section_E13 extends Section { /** * SPECIFICATION * 1. level 4 * 2. permissive * 3. closed * 4. first part is a Heading * 5. expects to be nested * * [D] "Having a canonical reference compels it to purity" * [D, EVENT 14] "purity is at composition level and composition returns parts * one level down" — integrated here because it is a direct clarification of * this member. Claude had called the closure "shallow" by asking whether * foreign content appears anywhere in the subtree; that is not the criterion. * A composition's contents are its parts, one level down, and a closed * Section's parts are pure. * * [D] "A permissive but closed composition allows all sorts of writing, but * nothing outside of it." */ readonly closed: true; } /** * PARAGRAPH, relative to a closed Section * [D] "making paragraph an emergent sort of letter of its own kind" * * Recorded as stated. No new member. Paragraph's own declaration is unchanged; * what changes is what it is relative to a closed Section above it. */ /** * THE 0-3 BAND * [D] "The first 0-3 levels are all like one intermixed level." * * Informal. Letter(0), Word(1), Sentence(2), Paragraph(3) read as one * intermixed level. The formal treatment is still four levels, and `parts` * follows the formal one. * * This is also the strongest evidence in the stream that Letter is level 0 * rather than 1 — see the OPEN note on Letter. */ /** * SPECIFICATION IS UNIVERSAL * [D] "This is all specified in its specification. All pieces of writing are * subject to their specification and we have been quietly building this" * * Every class in this file carries a specification. The machinery that RUNS * specifications — dev mode, assert mode, the level dial — arrives at * EVENT 20/21 and is not integrated yet. */ /* ============================================================================= * NEXT IN THE STREAM (Part 3) * ============================================================================= * - calligraphy named; parts corrected (it does not filter strings, and it * skips annotations) * - Parenthetical introduced on Writing; Annotation is the canonical * parenthetical; parenthetical controls not printing * - "parenthetical is on writing. level is on grading. Annotation is * parenthetical. Composition is not. Both can send and render those * properties non-reactive." * - the canonical is required; a Section without one is a void * - specification runs in dev mode only; assert mode; genome / phenotype * - the annotation collection: declared types, built at bond, dynamic list, * add/remove, unique, ordering * - Mention/Referent exported as Mentioned; Quote * - Reference exported as Means; Ref; the anchor/id pair * - Format; Theme * =========================================================================== */ ENDOFFILE wc -l writing-library.ts
Presented files
Part 2 is in. Notes on the winding:
The rename applied. subscript → level at EVENT 10, and your EVENT 12 example confirms it in use (<Composition open level=4>). Part 1's history block now records all three renames in sequence rather than leaving the member stale.
Two of my earlier readings got quarantined rather than deleted. The tail-as-back-matter correction now sits as a correction with your words, so an implementer sees what I had wrong and why it matters. Same with the "closure is shallow" business — I filed your purity clarification directly on Section.closed, since it's about that member, and marked what the criterion actually is.
One place I stopped myself. I'd claimed in conversation that openness makes Heading's wrap unnecessary. You didn't confirm or deny it, you moved to Section. So it's in the file as [inference — NOT Doug's statement] on Heading_E12, flagged specifically so nobody removes the wrap on my say-so.
New open item worth your eye: where open/closed lives. You said Composition evolves the pair, but the same sentence puts Letter in the range that can now take react — and Letter isn't a Composition. It's the same shape of question you answered crisply for parenthetical ("parenthetical is on writing, level is on grading"), so I've left it rather than guessing.
Also carried forward, still unanswered: Heading's depth relative to its Section, and Letter at 0 vs 1. Andmargin is named at EVENT 9 and never given anything.
Part 3 would run: calligraphy and the parts corrections, Parenthetical, the property split, dev/assert mode and the genome framing, then the annotation collection with unique and ordering. That's a natural block — it's where the specification machinery actually gets built. Say when.

Both files are below. What the separation showed:
The stream is 61 events, and the numbering is now canonical. Every EVENT n in the .ts resolves to an entry in the .events.md. That was the first thing the audit caught — I'd been counting your turns loosely for forward references, and one was off by eight (the formulator, cited as 45, is E53). The rest happened to line up.
The audit found twelve things, and they sort into three kinds. Three omissions — the Genesis sentence naming Writing's children, "null writing" on Letter, and the full naming history of the catalytic constructor. Three unmarked inferences that had crept into code rather than comments — the compose signature encoded which argument becomes the parent, bondConstructor was declared void, and the 0–3 band note asserted what parts does there. Five instances of my withdrawn vocabulary leaking back in as paraphrase: "leading/trailing region" for your "at the end" and "the tail," "no standing," "binding is the position," "declared not measured," "proposes structure." All fixed; the audit section lists each with its resolution.
The vocabulary leaks are the ones worth noting. They weren't in the quoted lines — those were clean. They were in my summaries of the quoted lines, where a withdrawn framing came back as a natural-sounding gloss. The stream document catches this because it holds your terms alone with nothing of mine in between, so a mismatch stands out.
One new flag from building the stream, for later: at E30 the verbatim word for Book is "closed and restrictive." I rendered it as strict in conversation and you didn't object, but you never said strict. Marked ⚠ in the events file for confirmation before that part.
Carried forward unresolved: Letter 0 vs 1, Heading's depth, where open/closed lives, margin, restrictive vs strict.

The implementation file is the compiled final form: 27 classes, each with declaration, history through the stream, notes, and specify() with every line cited. The compile is from the events file directly, so it goes all the way to E63 rather than stopping where the .ts does.
On the double-check. I re-read all 63 events against your messages before compiling and found nothing beyond the "restrictive" you'd already corrected. Two places the verbatim is ambiguous and I had to pick a reading — E22's "the mentioned one" and E33's "the table of contents for a synopsis is a type of chapter" — are named in the log so you can overrule them. The members that appear in the stream and don't appear in the compiled tree are listed with the event that removed them, so nothing quietly vanished.
Writing specify() surfaced five things that would ship wrong rather than merely unfinished. Letter's level, Heading's depth, and the same-class question on parts you've seen. Two are new from the compile: Title and Summary have no class — they're Chapter's required first two parts and I can't write their require line without knowing what they are — and unique add on the same class reads as a throw under a literal "proper subclass," which may or may not be what you meant.
Two consequences fell out of the rules that you never stated and might want to look at. Under the finalparts rule, a Section's Heading is a part — parts(section)[0] — because it's a Sentence, not an annotation, not the same class. And Book strict at 6 forces Cover, Synopsis and ToC to be Chapters, which agrees with everything you said about them but was never said as a constraint.
The three files are below. The .ts stays as the audited process record for E1–E13; this new file is what an implementer would build from.

Level is non-increasing downward and cannot climb back, so the same-class run on any path is contiguous — which is why a local check suffices `[consequence]`.""", """- **depth.** Recursion is same-type nesting; depth counts it. "Checked against one's parent" `[D:E10]` reads as a local check `[inference]`: same-type parent → `parent.depth + 1`, else 0. Level is non-increasing downward and cannot climb back, so the same-type run on any path is contiguous — which is why a local check suffices `[consequence]`. The rule is per class with that default: Heading takes its Section's depth instead `[D:E7]`, and Part takes its Book's plus one `[D:E31]`, so the assertion must be against the class's own rule, not the default (second pass).""") # ---- Fix B: "same type" — Part is-a Book requires instanceof; Book's no-parent line rep(""" - ⚠ "same type": same class exactly, or a subclass too? A subclassed Section inside a Section — unstated.""", """ - ⚠ "same type": same class exactly, or a subclass too? Unstated — but not free. Part is the Book that goes in a book and enumerates chapters at different depths `[D:E31]`. For that to fall out, Book > Part must be a same-type nesting: it must splice in `parts` and increment `depth`. Exact-class cannot deliver it (Part ≠ Book); `instanceof` can (Part is-a Book). The same holds for a subclassed Section inside a Section. The properties argue for `instanceof` `[inference]` — or for reading E31 as Part being the *role* of any Book at depth ≥ 1, with no subclass at all (open item 16).""") rep(""" if b is not a Part: assert b.parent is none -- E35""", """ assert (b.parent is none) == (b.depth == 0) -- E35 (the top has no parent); E31 (a nested Book is a Part)""") rep("""``` specify(p): Book.specify(p) minus the no-parent assertion require p.parent is a Book -- E31 assert p.depth == p.parent.depth + 1 -- E31 [consequence of Composition depth] ```""", """``` specify(p): Book.specify(p) -- its no-parent line reads (parent none) == (depth 0) require p.parent is a Book -- E31 assert p.depth == p.parent.depth + 1 -- E31; the default depthRule with same type = instanceof ```""") rep("""**Notes.** Same class as Book, so it nests and depth counts it `[consequence of E7/E10]`; that is what makes nested tables of contents fall out rather than needing a mechanism `[D:E31]`. If Book were permissive, a Part would be indistinguishable from a large chapter `[consequence]`.""", """**Notes.** Same type as Book, so it nests and depth counts it `[consequence of E7/E10]`; that is what makes nested tables of contents fall out rather than needing a mechanism `[D:E31]`. If Book were permissive, a Part would be indistinguishable from a large chapter `[consequence]`. ⚠ **Subclass or role.** "A Part will finally be the Book that goes in a book" `[D:E31]` admits two readings: a subclass `Part extends Book`, or simply the name for any Book whose parent is a Book (depth ≥ 1), with no class of its own. Presented files
The second pass earned its keep. Starting from properties instead of rules, and reading the files rather than remembering them, turned up four places where the first-pass specify() pseudo-code was internally inconsistent — all now fixed and logged:
Heading's depth contradicted Composition's. Heading extends Sentence, so it inherited Composition's default assertion that its depth is 0 (its parent is a Section, not a Sentence run) and then asserted it equals the Section's. Depth is now a per-class rule with a default; Heading and Part state their own.
Part broke under exact-class "same type." This is the important one, because it turns an open question into a decided one. Part is the Book that goes in a book and enumerates chapters at different depths (E31). For that to fall out, Book > Part has to be a same-type nesting — splice in parts, increment depth. Exact-class can't deliver it, since Part ≠ Book. instanceof can. So the "same type" question (open item 4) isn't free; the nested-ToC property answers it. Book's no-parent line now reads (parent is none) == (depth == 0) so it holds for every nested Book.
TableOfContents used 0. With Parts spliced, their chapters would arrive flattened and the ToC couldn't nest them. It now walks the body — which also makes the nice symmetry explicit: ToC and parts are the two projections of the same body, structure kept and structure dropped.
Theme asserted on "non-tail" children. Formats are Annotations, so a Theme's children are all tail. Empty assertion. Now it asserts on every child.
The new Properties → guarantees section sits before the classes and does what your rephrasing asked: each property we decided a class has, what must hold for it, and which open item threatens it. Five open items threaten a stated property; the rest are choices. One new item, 16 — E31 admits Part as either a subclass or simply the role of any Book at depth ≥ 1, and both give nested ToCs.
File below.

Calligraphy isn't in the object model. When I gave attributes, I designed them in boolean pairs so that either could be put on the the React
<Composition open /> <Composition closed />
Try to note what is props
I want you to think in terms of what comes into writing: ...$Chemical[]
A $Block is a chemical that holds a collection of inline html elements, strings and numbers as part of the react tree, but otherwise it is $Html. A React function component will be $Func<typeof Component> I believe.
We need to sort through that. We have string and permissive. So imagine that we sort that, pull out the writing. And amongst those, if there are annotations, they can only be in the tail.
We might imagine putting these things in collections.
annotations: Annotation[] is the obvious on Writing
And then maybe: $ contents: Chemical[]
For a permissive piece of writing, it might be hard to separate the React from the chemicals in text, but Composition might further specify that
Does this help you have something to hold onto? THere's a lot of specify there and a lot to do so efficiently. I want clear code. Class
Member
Notes
specify is a member of every piece of writing, and it's property inheritance
Hints: You do not cache things. This is not a place for that. Computed properties make a lot more sense, so that everything flows from source
You might call: source: $Chemical[] contents: $Chemical[] annotations: $Annotation[]
Where annotations and contents are computed from source and source is exactly what comes in the bond constructor put into an array
Composition will add: parts: $Writing[]
That will further filter the contents for just the writing inside

This view sharpens a lot. Reading it back before building on it:
Source is the one stored thing. source: $Chemical[] is exactly what the bond constructor received. contents, annotations, parts are computed from it every time — nothing cached, everything flows from source.
Calligraphy is not in the model. What I'd been calling calligraphy is $Block | $Html | $Func — chemicals that are not $Writing. The model sorts source by type; "calligraphy" stays a word about what's left after you pull out the writing.
The tail rule is on the writing subsequence. Sort source, pull out the writing; among those, annotations only in the tail. Non-writing chemicals are not subject to it.
Props are the declared attributes, in boolean pairs. <Composition open /> or <Composition closed />; strict/permissive the same. Many values I'd modelled as props are actually source — <Type>Autobiography</Type> reads its name from source, <Of>[Math](/path)</Of> reads its link from source.
0 is a member of every Writing, inherited, run in test mode, written as named tests about what source must satisfy.
Applying to all three. The .ts becomes the compiled object model in clear code (the Parts 1–2 process version is kept aside aswriting-library.process.ts, since the stream now carries what its _E suffixes carried).

Assigned, not overridden (E7). Non-increasing downward, which is what makes same-type runs contiguous and depth locally checkable. * `strict` / `permissive` — prop pair * `<Composition strict>` or `<Composition permissive>` (E6, E64); one stored, the other its opposite. Permissive: child level ≤ n. Strict: child level ∈ {n−1, n} (E6). Permissive consults only the relation; strict consults the distance — nobody uses the numbers unless they need strict (E6). Default rule: same-type parent → parent's + 1, else 0 `[inference]`. Per class with that default — Heading takes its Section's (E7), Part its Book's + 1 (E31) — so the test is against the class's own rule. On the body, not on spliced parts (same level anyway). * `depth is checked against the parent` — E10. * `composition is not parenthetical` — E16. --- **Word · Sentence · Paragraph** (E6) — levels 1, 2, 3; permissive; open (E12) * Read as one intermixed level (E13); declared as three. The semantic family — informal names at a level (E6) — has weight under parts: two names at one level are two types and do not splice into each other `[consequence]`. Presented files
Yes, it helped — it changed the model in three places I'd had wrong, not just the presentation.
What the source-first view corrected. The tail rule had been on raw children, so a $Block sitting after an annotation would have failed it; it's now on the writing subsequence of source, as you said. A Type's name, an Of's link, a Means's key, and About/By's links had all been modelled as props — they're source: <Type>Autobiography</Type> reads its label from its first $Block. And "calligraphy" had crept into specify() as though the model tested for it; it now sorts by $Writing and the word stays in the "about the model" list.
The compiled model (writing-library.ts) is now real TypeScript and type-checks under strict. source is the one stored array; contents, annotations, parts, depth, body are getters. Props are marked, boolean pairs implemented as one stored field with the other computed as its opposite. specify() is inherited: each class calls super.specify() and adds named tests, and replaces one by naming it again — that's my reading of "property inheritance," marked as such. Every member cites its event; every cited event exists in the stream.
The notes are now Class → member → nested notes, with the why for each test, and the third pass logged.
One thing this view made clearer than before: body — the direct writing children, unspliced — turned out to be load-bearing. Canonical-first tests need it (a nested Section would otherwise surface its own Heading through parts), and the ToC needs it to keep depth. It's my member, marked [inference]; it may want a name of yours.
Three files below, plus writing-library.process.ts kept aside.

13:44 UTC, 20 September 2026
So what is the writing API for modifying the source? We know that the specify function enumerates them and calls a method they have called specifically:
annotation.specifically(writing)
Which allows it to apply its specification
This is the way in which annotations are like a genome.
So what do we need to do? Recall that we might want to: add an annotation - it can go to the end of source replace an annotation- finds the first instance of the annotation type and replaces it ensure an annotation- adds it if not present, replaces parent class, does nothing if a subclass is there remove an annotation - remove all instances of a type find annotations - find all instances of an annotation of a certain type
We want this API but we want to have as much happen in specify as we can, so we can do unsafe things in the bond constructor!
Do we use a special collection for source so that all of that can be made efficient? Is source protected, so that all modifications have to go through the writing methods?
This is the next thing I might help you with
I want you to read again, but focus on $Composition this time. It adds a lot. What does it add. How does it build on $Writing's foundation? Can you see similar patterns? What must it specify?
Claude
13:49 UTC, 20 September 2026
Reading E65 back, the principle underneath the five operations is the one that decides everything else:the bond constructor is unsafe and 0 carries the guarantees. So the API doesn't check anything — add pushes, replace swaps, ensure does its three cases, remove and find scan by type — and the annotation's own specifically(writing) is where uniqueness and placement get tested. That's the genome: each annotation brings its own piece of the host's specification.
It also supersedes three earlier things, which I've recorded: E26's front-by-default and its four modes (add now goes to the end; the modes become named methods), and E22's throw (ensure replaces a parent class instead — which resolves the same-class question, since "a subclass is there" includes the class itself).
On your two questions, my view: source protected, yes — it's the one stored thing, and if anything can splice it directly the computed views stop meaning anything; the bond constructor is inside the class and keeps its freedom. Special collection, not yet — the five operations are instanceof scans, the count is small (E26), and an index would be a cache (E64). If one arrives later it should be a thin typed wrapper over the array with exactly these five operations — which matters because of what I found reading Composition.

Whether "type" in these five means the class or the named type is open. protected bondConstructor(...children: $Chemical[]): void { this.source = [...children]; // (E64) for (const T of (this.constructor as typeof Writing).annotationTypes) this.ensure(new T()); // built at bond (E21); ensure, so an authored one is not doubled [inference] }""") rep(""" test("declared annotations were built", () => // (E21) (this.constructor as typeof Writing).annotationTypes.every(T => this.annotations.some(a => a instanceof T))); test("unique annotations occur once", () => // (E21, E22) this.annotations.every(a => !a.unique || this.annotations.filter(b => b.name === a.name).length === 1)); } }""", """ test("declared annotations were built", () => // (E21) (this.constructor as typeof Writing).annotationTypes.every(T => this.annotations.some(a => a instanceof T))); for (const a of this.annotations) a.specifically(this); // each annotation applies its specification to its writing (E65) — the genome // does not descend into contents; [inference] the test runner walks the tree ⚠ } }""") # tailStart no longer used rep(""" protected get tailStart(): number { // [inference] where the annotation list begins in source const i = this.source.findIndex(isAnnotation); Presented files
Reading Composition again with that focus, here's what I see.
What it adds. A grade (level), two admission modes on it, an openness to non-writing, a body (parts), a position within its own kind (depth), parenthetical fixed false — and, from E34, canonicals: the part whose subject proxies all the others, with the literal levels 0–3 having none.
How it builds on Writing. It continues the same sort one step further. Writing sorts source into contents and annotations — body and tail. Composition sorts contents into parts and the rest — the writing and what isn't. Everything it computes is a further filter of what Writing already computed. Nothing stored twice.
The pattern. This is the part I'd want you to look at. Writing has, on the tail: a declared collection (annotationTypes) built at bond, a computed list, five operations by type, and annotation.specifically(writing). Composition looks like it has the same four things on the body:
canonicalTypes — declared, must come first in order. I've added this one to the model (marked as mine) because it directly replaces Section's and Book's hand-written canonical tests with one inherited test against the declaration. That's the same relation annotationTypes has to "declared annotations were built."
parts — computed, as annotations is.
the five operations on parts — not stated, but ensure(TableOfContents) in Book's bond constructor would be the first real use: the one canonical nobody authors.
canonical.specifically(composition) — not stated either, but the canonical means its composition (E7) and its subject is its parent (E34); Heading's "I am first and my parent is a Section" already has exactly this shape.
What it must specify. The two pairs are opposites; closed ⇒ source is all writing; every writing in the body admitted by level under the current mode; depth agrees with the parent; not parenthetical; canonicals first, in order.
Two things the API itself raised. Protecting source immediately caught Referent reading its host's source for the key — it now reads contents, which is also the right thing. And Types differ by name, not class: find(Type) returns every Type, and ensure(new Type("Cover")) is a no-op if any Type is present, so Cover uses add. What "type" means in the five operations for a Type is the one question the API leaves.
The stream has E65, the model type-checks, and the notes carry the reading above under Composition. Open item 5 is resolved; three new ones — whether specify descends, "type" for Type, and the body-side pattern.

I don't want you making up terminology. If it didn't come from me, we discuss what sort of genesis I was imagining. Can you go back and find all the places where you inserted members? Semantics are very important here

Presented files
The sweep is in the notes as its own section, "Inserted by Claude — genesis undiscussed," and each item is marked in the model with that exact phrase — a separate category from [inference], because they're different failures. An inference is my reading of something you said. An insertion has nothing behind it.
Two were worse than inventions. admission you caught. body I found: it's your word for parts ("Parts is on composition. The body." E27), and I'd used it for a member of mine — the unspliced direct children. A collision hijacks your semantics rather than adding to them. Withdrawn; the need it filled is now a marked helper, directWriting, not a member, until it has a genesis.
Twelve inserted members, in the inventory with the need each answered and your nearest words. The ones I'd flag as most consequential: annotationTypes (your "a collection of types, which constructors for annotations," E21 — and "type" has since become Type the annotation, so my name got worse over time), canonicalTypes (my extrapolation of that shape onto parts), parent (your word, but its placement on $Chemical is mine — possibly $Chemistry's), by/about on Book, resolve(), and the two ToC placeholders. Plus a list of your nouns I rendered as accessors — heading, cover, key, link — not inventions, but listed so they can be renamed together if you have other names.
Concept words too, since that's how admission survived: floor, host, material reading, grade as a noun — all removed. The stream's vocabulary section now tracks withdrawals by event.
The one genesis question I'd put first, because a correctness test hangs on it: the canonical is the "first member" (E7). When a Section's first child is a Section, parts splices it and parts[0] is the child's Heading — so a void Section would pass. Either the direct children have a name of yours, or the answer is that splicing doesn't cross a canonical. I don't want to pick.

Presented files
The side-by-side found more than I expected, and the stream now has its own section listing every loss by event so you can check the check.
The reinterpretation that matters most. Open item 1 — "Letter level 0 or 1" — had been carried as a contradiction for four passes. Read against your actual sentence, E12 isn't a contradiction; it's the event where Letter moves to 1 so that text, React and strings can be level 0 and permissive can admit them. That was the fix for Heading manufacturing a Letter. It also explains why open/closed exists alongside permissive: permissive admits at-or-below, so every composition would admit level 0 — closed is the switch that says not here. The model now has Letter at 1 sharing with Word, text as the implicit 0, and the reading marked as awaiting your confirmation.
$Block extends $Html — you said "otherwise it is $Html" and I'd made them siblings.
E22, unresolved: "the Mention has a parent called a Reference" — I modelled siblings; "parent" may mean superclass. Open item 20.
What the compression had dropped, in two grades. Mechanism phrases: "Text then specifies" (specifying is the verb of evolution), "much like level is set as a prop," "this is where the compositional power of the writing lives," "semantic structure is key," "they specify a type," "this is the CMS system." Framing: the E1 purpose statement, the level-0 Composition in E4's example, the E18 consent rule, "the document level" and the flexible-version choice at E29, and E44's "the canonical is the thing that was written from a perspective." All restored to their entries.
The pattern behind the losses: I compressed toward consequences for declarations and let go of sentences that carry semantics without naming a member. "Text then specifies" is the clearest case — it's the whole evolutionary mechanism in three words, and it produced no member, so it fell out.
Three files below; the model type-checks.

Presented files
To your three questions, plainly: no, I can't articulate all the properties. What I can do is say exactly where the edges are. The properties I can state are the ones with a sentence of yours behind them; the ones I can't are places where I know a need exists — a test or a bond constructor reaches for something — and I filled it with a name of mine. Those are the inserted members, and there are also a few needs I haven't filled at all because I couldn't see the shape (how a Canonical finds where it goes; what a ToC entry is; how a Means modifies a Format at render).
So yes, I want the series. Nine classes, listed in the notes under "Members Claude cannot supply," ordered so that answers propagate downward. One at a time. First:
Writing.
Context. At E21 Writing gains "the idea that it contains a collection [of] types, which constructors for annotations, and in its bond, it builds them and adds them to the annotation list." Subclasses "can specify the ones they need." At E64 source became the one stored thing, and annotations is computed from it; at E65 the bond constructor uses ensure to put the built ones into source. So there are two things on Writing that every subclass touches: the declared collection — the constructors — and the way a subclass says which it needs. I named the first annotationTypes and made it a static array; I modelled the second as a subclass overriding that static. Neither name nor mechanism is yours, and "type" has since become Type the annotation, which makes my name actively misleading.
Also on Writing, smaller: attributes come in pairs so either can be given (E64). parenthetical is a single boolean and its opposite is unnamed.
The ask. What is the declared collection of annotation constructors called, and how does a subclass specify the ones it needs — a static, a method it overrides, a declaration in the specification, something else? And does parenthetical have a partner?
Doug
17:14 UTC, 20 September 2026
"I named the first annotationTypes and made it a static array; I modelled the second as a subclass overriding that static. Neither name nor mechanism is yours, and "type" has since become Type the annotation, which makes my name actively misleading." - no! All annotations are like types. And there's no fixed set of them
Claude
17:15 UTC, 20 September 2026
Understood — two corrections, and the second one is structural.
Annotations are types. My worry about the name was wrong; that's the design (E20 "like a genome", E26 "they specify a type", E32 "we will use annotations to type"). Type the annotation is one type among annotations-that-are-types.
No fixed set. That kills the mechanism, not just the name. A static array per class is a fixed set. What E21 says is that in its bond a writing builds annotations and adds them to the list, and the list is dynamic. So there's nothing to declare: a subclass builds what it needs in its bond constructor through the API, and tests for what it needs in specify. The collection of types is the annotation list.

Presented files
annotationTypes and its test are gone from all three. Writing's bond constructor now does one thing — puts the children into source — and each subclass builds what it needs after that through the API. Cover, Quote and Ref already worked that way; the base class was the only place the fixed-set assumption lived.
I've carried the parenthetical partner as still open rather than re-asking it.
Question 2, and it's the one that a correctness test hangs on:
Composition.
Context. Three things I reached for and named myself.
The direct children. "The section has a canonical first member called a Heading" (E7). parts is the body (E27), and a same-type child is flattened in (E7, E26). So when a Section's first child is itself a Section, parts[0] is the child's Heading — and a Section with no Heading of its own would pass a test written on parts. The canonical test, and the ToC's walk for nested Parts (E31), both need the children before splicing. I first called that body, which is your word for parts, then made it a marked helper, directWriting. It has no name of yours.
The canonicals as a set. Section requires Heading first (E7); Chapter requires Title then Summary (E30); Book requires Cover, Synopsis, Table of Contents (E30); "the canonical pattern continues upward." Unlike annotations, theseare a fixed set per class. I made a static canonicalTypes so one inherited test could check "canonicals first, in order." That's my mechanism.
The pattern on parts. Writing has add / replace / ensure / remove / find on its annotations (E65). I could see the same five wanting to exist on parts — ensure(TableOfContents) in Book's bond would be the first use, the one canonical nobody authors — and possibly a canonical.specifically(composition) mirroring annotation.specifically(writing). Pattern seen; nothing stated.
The ask. What are the direct, unspliced writing children called — or is the answer that a same-type child can't sit in the canonical's position at all? How does a composition know its canonicals — a declared set, or only each class's own specification? And do the five operations exist on parts?

You're right, and here is the shape of what I don't understand, before the questions.
Where identity lives. I modelled every canonical as a class at a position: Cover extends Chapter, tested as the first part. Your sentence says Cover is an annotation, applied to a chapter, and the book finds its cover by it. That's a different design: the role is conferred by an annotation and located by search. If that's the design, then Heading, Title, Summary, Synopsis, TableOfContents and maybe Part are the same, and every inserted member around canonicals — canonicalTypes, directWriting, the position tests — was me solving a problem the design doesn't have. I don't know whether it is the design. Most of my insertions came from this one confusion.
The $Chemistry boundary. I don't know what a chemical is given and what it must supply — what arrives at the bond constructor, who sets source, whether parent exists, how anything renders. I filled those in.
So, questions. Numbered so you can answer by number.
A. Canonicals as annotations
Is Cover the Type with name "Cover" (<Type>Cover</Type>, E51), or its own annotation class?
"Can only be applied to a chapter" — is that Cover's specifically(writing) testing that the writing is a Chapter?
"The book can find its cover with it" — does the book search its parts for the chapter carrying Cover? Is that search a member on Composition, or does the book walk parts and call find(Cover) on each?

Are Synopsis and TableOfContents also annotations applied to chapters, found the same way?
Is Heading a class (E7 "Heading: Sentence"), or a Sentence carrying a Heading annotation? Found by annotation, or is "first member" still positional?
Title and Summary — annotations applied to some writing inside the chapter? Applied to what — a Sentence, a Section, anything?
Is Part an annotation applied to a nested Book?
Two Sentences in one Section both carrying Heading — is that caught by uniqueness in Heading's specifically, or by something else?
Is there a way to ask any composition "what is your canonical," or does each class ask for its own (cover, heading, title)?
B. Lifting
Is "lift" a member? On Book, or on Writing? E57 said "import upward" — same operation, and which word is the name?
Does lifting copy or move? Is the By still on the cover afterward?
"The annotations it wants" — By, About, the Types? Chosen in the bond constructor?
"Do whatever with them" — E35 said a parent may translate. Is translating a member, or just what the bond does?
Does a lift go through add, ensure or replace?
C. What Writing gets from $Chemistry
When my bond constructor runs, are the children already constructed chemicals, or are they constructed during my bond (E54 "transduced")?
Does $Chemistry set source from the children before my bond runs, or do I set it?
Does the bond constructor return anything?
Does every chemical know its parent? Is that $Chemistry's?
How does a chemical render what it holds — a method I write, or does $Chemistry render from source?
What is reactive — props? source? Do the computed getters re-run when source changes?
What is $Block's collection called?
Are $Writing and $Annotation real $Chemistry types, or your notation for "chemicals that are Writing"?
D. Writing and the API
Does parenthetical have a partner name?
find(T) when T is Type and Types differ by name — find all Types then filter, or a find by name?
Is an annotation's name a prop, or class-level?
Does specify descend into contents, or does something walk the tree calling it on each writing?
Can a subclass replace an inherited test by naming it again, or only add?
What is a void in production — a Section without its Heading renders nothing? Throws? Renders as plain writing?
How many modes are there — production, dev, test/validation, assert — and which one runs specify?
E. Composition
"Same type" for splicing: does a subclass count (instanceof), or exact class only?
Does depth count same-type nesting or same-level nesting?
Is depth computed, or sent down like level and parenthetical (E16)?
Heading's depth — its Section's, or one more?
Do add / replace / ensure / remove / find exist on parts?

"The Mention has a parent called a Reference" — a superclass, so Referent extends Reference?

G. Canonical, the Letter
Authored by the writer, or inserted by the compiler?

H. Type, Of, Quote, Format, Theme
Type's name comes from its source string — right?

Autobiography and Biography — Type names, or classes?
Quote is a Sentence? Ref is a Word?

Is Format unique?

I. Table of contents and the compiler

Does the ToC read its book at bond time or at render?
Does the compiler run before bonding (on TSX) or after?
What does it produce for a <Means> — a path, an id, something else?

Claude
21:09 UTC, 20 September 2026
Held the artifacts — nothing from this is integrated until you rule on the flagged items, since several of them are the team reporting your words and I can't tell a ruling from a paraphrase.
Where the team departs from the Genesis
1. Title and Summary — misattributed, and two rulings that can't both hold. A.7 says "Title and Summary are classes, as the Genesis has them." The Genesis never gave them classes; that was open item 3 for six passes. Your choosing classes in the room is a ruling — fine — but the team then cites you from 2026-09-15: "The chapter title IS the heading of its first section PERIOD." That predates the Genesis and contradicts E30, where a chapter's first two parts are a title and a summary. If the title is the first section's heading, the chapter's first part is a Section, and the summary has nowhere stated to sit. One of these is canon. E30 is in the Genesis; the 9/15 line isn't.
2. Heading "not by position." A.6 says Heading is found by kind, "not by position." E7 says "the section has a canonical first member called a Heading." Finding by kind is compatible with testing first; dropping first isn't. And they keep the order test for Book (A.4, "E30's order is still a rule the book tests") and for Chapter, but not for Section. The asymmetry has no basis in the stream.
3. Canonical is a Letter, not an empty mention. G.1 makes the canonical mark "a mention written with no words." E33 says it "appears as a type of nameless Letter that serves as a suffix." A mention is an Annotation; annotations are parenthetical — hidden, by ruling 6. The canonical mark is printed: it's "the mark that brings you from … synopsis entry to book." Putting it in the annotation hierarchy hides the one thing the reader is meant to see. The Genesis puts it under Letter, and the reason is visible.
4. "A member on Composition that searches parts by kind." A.3 carries this from the first library. E70 says the book finds its cover with the annotation. By kind — the part's class — can't find a Cover, since every cover is a Chapter; it has to be by the annotation carried. The team's sentence says "the chapter carrying Cover … through a member that searches by kind," which is either two searches or one that isn't the Genesis's. And it's an unnamed member imported from the source — your rule about liberties applies to them as much as to me. Same for their own vocabulary: worn, kind, synthesis, specimen, read/follow (they flag the last as offered; the rest they don't).
5. Modes. D.7 says three modes on one flag, NODE_ENV !== 'production'. That's binary. E20 wanted dev fast and the slow validation held elsewhere; E21 wanted the specification flagged to mode. A binary can't separate dev from test.
6. They don't have E68 or E69. Their stream stops at E65. Ruling 3's "levels 1 to 3" reads correctly only on E68's numbering (Letter 1, Word 2, Sentence 3 — the ones "beneath prose"); on the numbering they hold, Paragraph would be authored-when-wanted, which a closed Section forbids. Anything they've reasoned from Letter at 0 is stale. And they don't have E69's "no fixed set."
7. Referent extends Reference — from the source, not the stream. E22's "parent" supports a superclass, so this isn't a misreading. But semantically a referent (what is pointed at) being a kind of reference (what points) is backwards unless a mention is understood as a reference to here. Worth your eye since it came from the first library's shape.
What surprised me most
0 is the class. Writing is the component the export pattern makes so <Writing> can be written. Your $Writing[] at E64 was the class name all along; I took it for notation. And $-prefixed fields are props — so $level, $open, $narrative — while plain fields are reactive. My model has every prop unprefixed.
The table of contents is authored. I'd assumed it was the one canonical nobody writes. Your question — "how are you validating all chapters in the TOC if some of them don't have titles?" — only makes sense if it's written and checked.
0 is a formula. The name inside swaps in the subclass filed under it. The formulator resolves Type names. That's what makes "one annotation, two spellings" work.
"A chemical is born its own parent." So a top-level book's parent is itself, not nothing — my no-parent test is wrong as written.
Why the first library stopped splicing. Drawing from the spliced reading dissolved every nested section. The Genesis's answer is contents — draw from the unspliced children, splice only in parts. That's the thing I'd been reaching for with directWriting, and it was already there.
The parse is gone. The first library parsed prose into sentences and words. Levels beneath prose are authored when wanted; otherwise calligraphy. E14 and E32 said this; I hadn't understood there was a parser to remove.
What it clarifies
Of the open items: Heading's depth is its Section's (2); same type is instanceof (4); a void is nothing in production (9); Of names a Book (10); Autobiography and Biography are classes (12); styled-components, with Format (15); Part is a subclass (16); specify doesn't descend — a scaffolded suite walks (17); named Types are classes, so find-by-class is find-by-name (18); no operations on parts, reading only (19). The parenthetical partner is narrative, and parenthetical means hidden rather than not rendered.
Of my inserted members: parent is the framework's; items is elements; canonicalTypes and directWriting dissolve into contents; test() is the suite; by/about become each class asking for its own; resolve is offered as read. Lifting is not a member — it's what the book's bond does, through add, copying.
Two things I'd want before integrating: your ruling on Title (E30 or 9/15), and whether the canonical mark stays a Letter. The rest I can apply.

21:40 UTC, 20 September 2026
Applying all seven and the naming rule. On the Summary question — yes, sensible, with one constraint the framework imposes: a chemical has one parent (E71 C.4), so the constructed summary can't be the first piece of writing; it has to be a copy of it or a mention of it. A mention keeps the summary true when the paragraph is edited, and a Summary is parenthetical by default, which is what a mention-that-draws-its-target wants to be. Second, smaller: if the first non-title writing is a Section, "constructed from it" could mean the section or its first paragraph. I've written it as constructing from that writing and flagged both.
The naming rule changes every line of the model, so this is a rewrite rather than a patch.

Presented files
All seven rulings and the naming rule are in. What changed structurally:
The canonical is now a member on every composition — the first element — and each level names it, specifies its kind, and lets a subclass override by returning a new value for the named getter, with canonical reading through it. That single mechanism replaces canonicalTypes, directWriting and the position tests; the unspliced reading was contents all along. Section: the canonical is a heading. Chapter: the canonical is a title, "need not be a section." Book: the canonical is the cover — first, and carrying $Cover, found by searching its annotations by type name. No search-parts member; parts are compositional.
Cover, Synopsis and Table of Contents are 0 subclasses applied to chapters. <Type>Cover</Type> resolves to $Cover through the formulator. The earlier class Cover extends Chapter is gone.
0 and 1 are classes. Summary is optional and constructed in the bond when absent, marked with the one-parent constraint. Title's superclass and level are the main thing still open.
Referent is gone; 0 and 1, not Types. $Canonical stays a Letter that means things related to its parent. parenthetical/narrative. specify is on in test mode, not dev — E20's "dev" is superseded in the notes.
The 0 naming rule is applied throughout: $Writing, $level, $open; the components and JSX props drop it. The model type-checks.
Still open after this pass: Title's superclass and level; whether $Mention extends $Reference (E22's "parent" — the first library has it); copy versus mention for the constructed summary; $Author under $Autobiography is my reading of E52 with the team's H.3; by/about on Book remain marked as mine. Everything the team said that you didn't contradict is carried as[team], distinct from your rulings.
